It's like... well, it's like I don't get to make the decision anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at other times of the day, not just at night?\n\nPatient: During the day? Yeah, I think I'm mostly fine then. I work in shipping and receiving, so I stay pretty busy on my feet. I just eat my regular lunch and... well, I don't really think twice about it. I don't get that same feeling of not being able to stop. It really just seems to happen at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. On some nights after work, I can go through... well, a lot. I'll eat several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, some tortillas, and whatever rice or beans are left over. I do it all pretty fast, just standing there in the kitchen. It's almost like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers, but... yeah, it ends up being a really large amount of food in just an hour or so.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I feel pretty stuffed and uncomfortable physically, but... I wouldn't say I feel guilty or ashamed. I usually just finish up, wash the dishes, and go to bed. I haven't really noticed feeling bad about myself like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food at night by yourself, or is someone else with you?\n\nPatient: I'm by myself. I... well, I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. So I usually wait until my aunt and the rest of the house are asleep, and I just keep it quiet in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Does this nighttime eating pattern cause problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, work is fine. I'm still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts and handling my responsibilities there without any issues. But at home... I guess it does affect things a bit. I've actually started avoiding some of that late family time when everyone's hanging out in the kitchen. I just... I worry someone is going to notice how uneasy I am around the food. I don't get that same feeling of not being able to stop. It really just seems to happen at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. On some nights after work, I can go through... well, a lot. I'll eat several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, some tortillas, and whatever rice or beans are left over. I do it all pretty fast, just standing there in the kitchen. It's almost like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers, but... yeah, it ends up being a really large amount of food in just an hour or so.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I feel pretty stuffed and uncomfortable physically, but... I wouldn't say I feel guilty or ashamed. I usually just finish up, wash the dishes, and go to bed. I haven't really noticed feeling bad about myself like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food at night by yourself, or is someone else with you?\n\nPatient: I'm by myself. I... well, I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. I'm still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts and handling my responsibilities there without any issues. But at home... I guess it does affect things a bit. It's almost like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers, but... yeah, it ends up being a really large amount of food in just an hour or so.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I feel pretty stuffed and uncomfortable physically, but... I wouldn't say I feel guilty or ashamed. I usually just finish up, wash the dishes, and go to bed. I haven't really noticed feeling bad about myself like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food at night by yourself, or is someone else with you?\n\nPatient: I'm by myself. I... well, I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. So I usually wait until my aunt and the rest of the house are asleep, and I just keep it quiet in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Does this nighttime eating pattern cause problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, work is fine. I'm still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts and handling my responsibilities there without any issues. But at home... I guess it does affect things a bit. I've actually started avoiding some of that late family time when everyone's hanging out in the kitchen. I just... I worry someone is going to notice how uneasy I am around the food. So I kind of just keep to myself until they clear out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually the part that worries me. Once I start picking at the food late at night, I'll tell myself, 'That's enough, Santiago.' But... it's like my hand just keeps going back for more. It really feels like I don't get to make the decision anymore once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control how much you're eating during the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really during the day. When I'm at work or just going about my normal routine, I eat a regular amount. I can stop when I'm full. It's really just at night when I'm alone that I feel like I lose that control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this nighttime eating is affecting your overall well-being or causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely bothers me. I mean, it's frustrating to feel like I can't just be normal around food in my own house at night. I don't know if I'd use the word significant, or say it's ruining my life or anything, since I'm still getting by and going to work just fine. But... it definitely weighs on my mind. I just feel pretty guilty and, I guess, ashamed after it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat it pretty fast. It's not like... well, not like when I'm sitting down for a regular meal with my family. It's more like I'm just trying to get it done before anyone wakes up. I'm going so quick I'm barely even tasting the food, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control how much you're eating during the day?\n\nPatient: I think you actually just asked me about that. But no, like I mentioned a minute ago, I don't have any issues during the day. When I'm at work at the warehouse, I just eat a normal lunch and I'm fine. I can stop when I'm full without any problem. It really is just a nighttime thing for me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned earlier that you don't usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating at night, but then later said you do feel guilty and ashamed - could you clarify how you typically feel after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, did I say I didn't? Sorry, I must have gotten my words mixed up, or maybe I was talking about my regular meals during the day. But for the nighttime eating... yeah, I definitely do feel guilty and ashamed after it happens. It just makes me feel pretty bad about myself when I realize how much I just ate in the dark like that. So, yeah, the guilt is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think it's because I'm stressed or anything like that. I mean, my job at the warehouse is pretty steady, nothing too crazy. And I don't really feel anxious or bored before it happens. I just wake up and... I just feel like I need to eat. It's hard to explain, but it doesn't seem tied to any particular feeling or mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense urge to eat at night that you can't ignore?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's... it's like this really strong pull. I wake up and it's just right there, this feeling that I have to go to the kitchen and eat something. I can't really just roll over and go back to sleep, you know? The urge is just too strong to ignore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. I'm Santiago. I guess I'm here because I'm having some trouble with my eating. \n\nWhat really worries me is that once I start picking at food late at night, I just can't seem to stop. I'll actually tell myself, \"That's enough, Santiago,\" but my hand just keeps going back for more. It's like... well, it's like I don't get to make the decision anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at other times of the day, not just at night?\n\nPatient: During the day? Yeah, I think I'm mostly fine then. I work in shipping and receiving, so I stay pretty busy on my feet. I just eat my regular lunch and... well, I don't really think twice about it. I don't get that same feeling of not being able to stop. It really just seems to happen at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. On some nights after work, I can go through... well, a lot. I'll eat several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, some tortillas, and whatever rice or beans are left over. I do it all pretty fast, just standing there in the kitchen. It's almost like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers, but... yeah, it ends up being a really large amount of food in just an hour or so.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I feel pretty stuffed and uncomfortable physically, but... I wouldn't say I feel guilty or ashamed. I usually just finish up, wash the dishes, and go to bed. I haven't really noticed feeling bad about myself like that, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat these large amounts of food at night by yourself, or is someone else with you?\n\nPatient: I'm by myself. I... well, I don't really like anyone seeing how much I put away. So I usually wait until my aunt and the rest of the house are asleep, and I just keep it quiet in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Does this nighttime eating pattern cause problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, work is fine. I'm still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts and handling my responsibilities there without any issues. But at home... I guess it does affect things a bit. I've actually started avoiding some of that late family time when everyone's hanging out in the kitchen. I just... I worry someone is going to notice how uneasy I am around the food. So I kind of just keep to myself until they clear out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually the part that worries me. Once I start picking at the food late at night, I'll tell myself, 'That's enough, Santiago.' But... it's like my hand just keeps going back for more. It really feels like I don't get to make the decision anymore once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control how much you're eating during the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really during the day. When I'm at work or just going about my normal routine, I eat a regular amount. I can stop when I'm full. It's really just at night when I'm alone that I feel like I lose that control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this nighttime eating is affecting your overall well-being or causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely bothers me. I mean, it's frustrating to feel like I can't just be normal around food in my own house at night. I don't know if I'd use the word significant, or say it's ruining my life or anything, since I'm still getting by and going to work just fine. But... it definitely weighs on my mind. I just feel pretty guilty and, I guess, ashamed after it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these nighttime episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I eat it pretty fast. It's not like... well, not like when I'm sitting down for a regular meal with my family. It's more like I'm just trying to get it done before anyone wakes up. I'm going so quick I'm barely even tasting the food, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control how much you're eating during the day?\n\nPatient: I think you actually just asked me about that. But no, like I mentioned a minute ago, I don't have any issues during the day. When I'm at work at the warehouse, I just eat a normal lunch and I'm fine. I can stop when I'm full without any problem. It really is just a nighttime thing for me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned earlier that you don't usually feel guilty or ashamed after eating at night, but then later said you do feel guilty and ashamed - could you clarify how you typically feel after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, did I say I didn't? Sorry, I must have gotten my words mixed up, or maybe I was talking about my regular meals during the day. So, yeah, the guilt is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think it's because I'm stressed or anything like that. I mean, my job at the warehouse is pretty steady, nothing too crazy. And I don't really feel anxious or bored before it happens. I just wake up and... I just feel like I need to eat. It's hard to explain, but it doesn't seem tied to any particular feeling or mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense urge to eat at night that you can't ignore?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's... it's like this really strong pull. I wake up and it's just right there, this feeling that I have to go to the kitchen and eat something. I can't really just roll over and go back to sleep, you know? The urge is just too strong to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat at night because you're embarrassed about how much food you're consuming and don't want others to see it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's definitely a big part of it. I'm still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts and handling my responsibilities there without any issues. But at home... I guess it does affect things a bit. It really feels like I don't get to make the decision anymore once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control how much you're eating during the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really during the day. It's really just at night when I'm alone that I feel like I lose that control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this nighttime eating is affecting your overall well-being or causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely bothers me. I mean, it's frustrating to feel like I can't just be normal around food in my own house at night. I don't get that same feeling of not being able to stop. It really just seems to happen at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. On some nights after work, I can go through... well, a lot. I'll eat several tamales, a big bowl of pozole, some tortillas, and whatever rice or beans are left over. I do it all pretty fast, just standing there in the kitchen. It's almost like I'm just cleaning up the leftovers, but... yeah, it ends up being a really large amount of food in just an hour or so.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these nighttime eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I feel pretty stuffed and uncomfortable physically, but... I wouldn't say I feel guilty or ashamed. I usually just finish up, wash the dishes, and go to bed. I'm still getting to my shipping and receiving shifts and handling my responsibilities there without any issues. But at home... I guess it does affect things a bit. I mean, it's frustrating to feel like I can't just be normal around food in my own house at night. I don't know if I'd use the word significant, or say it's ruining my life or anything, since I'm still getting by and going to work just fine. But... it definitely weighs on my mind. I mean, it's frustrating to feel like I can't just be normal around food in my own house at night. I don't know if I'd use the word significant, or say it's ruining my life or anything, since I'm still getting by and going to work just fine. But... it definitely weighs on my mind.     "psychosocial_stressor": null,
```
    "additional_requirements": [
      "Consuming an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances",
      "Eating at night due to a sense of compulsion rather than hunger"
    ]
  }
}
```
